## Daniel 9:19 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_jcqa)

אֲדֹנָ֤⁠י ׀ שְׁמָ֨עָ⁠ה֙ אֲדֹנָ֣⁠י ׀ סְלָ֔חָ⁠ה אֲדֹנָ֛⁠י הַֽקֲשִׁ֥יבָ⁠ה וַ⁠עֲשֵׂ֖ה

**"Lord, hear! Lord, forgive! Lord, pay attention and act"**

The words translated as **hear**, **forgive**, **pay attention** and **act** are imperatives, but they communicate a request rather than a command. If the imperative does not communicate a request for your readers, then use a form in your language that communicates a request. Alternate translation: \[Lord, I ask you to hear! Lord, I beg you to forgive! Lord, I plead with you to pay attention and act]

See: Imperatives — Other Uses

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:19
* **Associated Articles:** Imperatives — Other Uses (ID: `33284@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Daniel 9:20 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_hd7d)

וְ⁠ע֨וֹד אֲנִ֤י מְדַבֵּר֙ וּ⁠מִתְפַּלֵּ֔ל וּ⁠מִתְוַדֶּה֙ חַטָּאתִ֔⁠י וְ⁠חַטַּ֖את עַמִּ֣⁠י יִשְׂרָאֵ֑ל וּ⁠מַפִּ֣יל תְּחִנָּתִ֗⁠י לִ⁠פְנֵי֙ יְהוָ֣ה אֱלֹהַ֔⁠י עַ֖ל הַר־קֹ֥דֶשׁ אֱלֹהָֽ⁠י

**"And I was still speaking and praying and confessing my sin and the sin of my people Israel and making my supplication fall to the face of Yahweh my God for the mountain of the holiness of my God"**

You can create a verse bridge for these two verses, 20–21, in order not to repeat the information “I was still speaking.” The UST models one way to do that.

See: Verse Bridges

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:20
* **Associated Articles:** Verse Bridges (ID: `33410@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Daniel 9:20 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_h6wk)

וְ⁠ע֨וֹד אֲנִ֤י מְדַבֵּר֙ וּ⁠מִתְפַּלֵּ֔ל וּ⁠מִתְוַדֶּה֙ חַטָּאתִ֔⁠י וְ⁠חַטַּ֖את עַמִּ֣⁠י יִשְׂרָאֵ֑ל וּ⁠מַפִּ֣יל תְּחִנָּתִ֗⁠י לִ⁠פְנֵי֙ יְהוָ֣ה אֱלֹהַ֔⁠י עַ֖ל הַר־קֹ֥דֶשׁ אֱלֹהָֽ⁠י

**"And I was still speaking and praying and confessing my sin and the sin of my people Israel and making my supplication fall to the face of Yahweh my God for the mountain of the holiness of my God"**

The words that the ULT translates as **And I was still speaking** indicate that Gabriel came to Daniel at the same time as when Daniel was praying. If it would be helpful to your readers, you could describe the time relationship more explicitly. Alternate translation: \[While I was still speaking and praying and confessing my sin and the sin of my people Israel and presenting my supplication before Yahweh my God for the holy mountain of my God]

See: Connect — Simultaneous Time Relationship

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:20
* **Associated Articles:** Connect — Simultaneous Time Relationship (ID: `33334@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Daniel 9:21 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_adqy)

וְ⁠הָ⁠אִ֣ישׁ גַּבְרִיאֵ֡ל אֲשֶׁר֩ רָאִ֨יתִי בֶ⁠חָז֤וֹן בַּ⁠תְּחִלָּה֙

**"and the man Gabriel whom I had seen in the vision at the beginning"**

Daniel is reintroducing **the man Gabriel**, whom he had described earlier, as a participant in the story. Your language may have its own way of reintroducing participants that you can use in your translation. Alternate translation: \[and Gabriel, the same man whom I had seen earlier in the vision]

See: Introduction of New and Old Participants

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:21
* **Associated Articles:** Introduction of New and Old Participants (ID: `33421@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gabriel (ID: `deity:Gabriel`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`)

## Daniel 9:21 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_vcnj)

וְ⁠הָ⁠אִ֣ישׁ גַּבְרִיאֵ֡ל

**"and the man Gabriel"**

The implication is that Gabriel was an angel, and Daniel calls him **the man** because Gabriel appeared in human form. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[and the angel Gabriel]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:21
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gabriel (ID: `deity:Gabriel`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`)

## Daniel 9:21 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_lojp)

כְּ⁠עֵ֖ת מִנְחַת־עָֽרֶב

**"at the time of the offering of the evening"**

The implication is that **the offering of the evening** was the sacrifice that the priests offered every day at that time. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[at the time when the priests presented the daily evening sacrifice]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:21
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gabriel (ID: `deity:Gabriel`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`)

## Daniel 9:22 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_frdf)

וַ⁠יָּ֖בֶן וַ⁠יְדַבֵּ֣ר עִמִּ֑⁠י וַ⁠יֹּאמַ֕ר

**"And he made me understand and he spoke with me and said"**

Daniel is describing one event, Gabriel making him understand, before describing another event that preceded it, Gabriel speaking with him. If it would be helpful in your language, you may wish to relate these events in the order in which they happened, as in the UST.

See: Order of Events

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:22
* **Associated Articles:** Order of Events (ID: `33269@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`); Daniel (ID: `person:Daniel.3`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Daniel 9:22 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_i7j9)

לְ⁠הַשְׂכִּילְ⁠ךָ֥ בִינָֽה

**"to make you wise {for} understanding"**

If your language does not use an abstract noun for the idea of **understanding**, you could express the same idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[to enable you to understand wisely]

See: Abstract Nouns

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:22
* **Associated Articles:** Abstract Nouns (ID: `33257@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`); Daniel (ID: `person:Daniel.3`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Daniel 9:23 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_brjs)

יָצָ֣א דָבָ֗ר

**"a word came forth"**

The implication is that **a word came forth** from God. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[a word came forth from God]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:23
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Daniel 9:23 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_n8x6)

וַ⁠אֲנִי֙ בָּ֣אתִי

**"and I, I have come"**

Gabriel is stating the pronoun **I** separately, even though the verb translated as **I have come** already includes this meaning. He is doing that to emphasize that he himself has come immediately to answer Daniel’s prayer. If a speaker of your language would use an explicit pronoun for the same purpose, you may want to use that construction in your translation. If not, your language may have other ways of showing the meaning here. Alternate translation: \[I have indeed come]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:23
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Daniel 9:23 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_d8i6)

כִּ֥י חֲמוּד֖וֹת אָ֑תָּה

**"for you {are} desirablenesses"**

If your language does not use an abstract noun for the idea of **desirablenesses**, you could express the same idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[for you are very precious]

See: Abstract Nouns

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:23
* **Associated Articles:** Abstract Nouns (ID: `33257@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Daniel 9:23 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_e5s3)

כִּ֥י חֲמוּד֖וֹת אָ֑תָּה

**"for you {are} desirablenesses"**

Gabriel is using the term **desirablenesses** as a common expression of the culture to mean “one whom God loves greatly.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[for you are one whom God loves greatly]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:23
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Daniel 9:23 (#5) (id: en_tn_DAN_eggw)

וּ⁠בִין֙ בַּ⁠דָּבָ֔ר וְ⁠הָבֵ֖ן בַּ⁠מַּרְאֶֽה

**"So understand the word and understand the appearance"**

In the original language, the first **understand** is “Qal,” and the second **understand** is from “Hiphil.” The meaning of the second is not to “make understood” but ingressive, to “come to understand.” Some languages may have forms that they can use to express these slightly different meanings. Alternate translation: \[So consider this message and grasp the meaning of this vision] or \[Therefore, ponder the message and begin to understand the meaning of the vision]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:23
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Daniel 9:24 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_p1rm)

שָׁבֻעִ֨ים שִׁבְעִ֜ים נֶחְתַּ֥ךְ

**"Seventy sevens have been determined"**

The expression **have been determined** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[God has determined seventy sevens]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:24
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Smear (ID: `keyterm:Smear`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`)

## Daniel 9:24 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_md1q)

שָׁבֻעִ֨ים שִׁבְעִ֜ים

**"Seventy sevens"**

The expression **Seventy sevens** refers to seventy units of seven. Translate this number according to the conventions of your own language and culture. Alternate translation: \[Seventy periods of seven]

See: Numbers

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:24
* **Associated Articles:** Numbers (ID: `33392@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Smear (ID: `keyterm:Smear`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`)

## Daniel 9:24 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_nzce)

לְ⁠כַלֵּ֨א הַ⁠פֶּ֜שַׁע ו⁠ל⁠חתם חטאות וּ⁠לְ⁠כַפֵּ֣ר עָוֺ֔ן וּ⁠לְ⁠הָבִ֖יא צֶ֣דֶק עֹֽלָמִ֑ים

**"to finish transgression and to seal up sins and to cover iniquity and to bring in righteousness of eternities"**

If your language does not use abstract nouns for the ideas of **transgression**, **sins**, **iniquity**, and **righteousness**, you could express the same ideas in another way. Alternate translation: \[to stop people from transgressing and to seal up what they have done wrongly and to cover what they have done perversely and to bring in what is eternally right]

See: Abstract Nouns

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:24
* **Associated Articles:** Abstract Nouns (ID: `33257@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Smear (ID: `keyterm:Smear`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`)

## Daniel 9:24 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_bpmo)

ו⁠ל⁠חתם חטאות

**"and to seal up sins"**

Gabriel is speaking as if putting an end to sins were **to seal up** a container so that nothing more could come out of it. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[and to put an end to sins]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:24
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Smear (ID: `keyterm:Smear`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`)

## Daniel 9:24 (#5) (id: en_tn_DAN_hof7)

וּ⁠לְ⁠כַפֵּ֣ר עָוֺ֔ן

**"and to cover iniquity"**

Gabriel is speaking as if forgiving iniquity were **to cover** it so that it could no longer be seen. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[and to forgive iniquity]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:24
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Smear (ID: `keyterm:Smear`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`)

## Daniel 9:24 (#6) (id: en_tn_DAN_z8zj)

וְ⁠לַ⁠חְתֹּם֙ חָז֣וֹן וְ⁠נָבִ֔יא

**"and to seal up vision and prophecy"**

Gabriel is speaking as if bringing about what the prophets foretold were **to seal up** a document to certify it. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[and to fulfill vision and prophecy]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:24
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 9:24 (#7) (id: en_tn_DAN_n67t)

וְ⁠לִ⁠מְשֹׁ֖חַ קֹ֥דֶשׁ קָֽדָשִֽׁים

**"and to anoint the Holy of Holies"**

This could mean: (1\) **the Holy of Holies** refers to the most holy place in the temple or (2\) it refers to a most holy person. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[and to anoint the most holy place in the temple]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:24
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 9:25 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_zw7v)

וְ⁠תֵדַ֨ע וְ⁠תַשְׂכֵּ֜ל

**"And may you know and understand"**

The terms **know** and **understand** mean similar things. Gabriel is using the two terms together for emphasis. If it would be clearer for your readers, you could express the emphasis with a single phrase. Alternate translation: \[And may you understand this clearly]

See: Doublet

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:25
* **Associated Articles:** Doublet (ID: `33266@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Daniel 9:25 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_koms)

מִן־מֹצָ֣א דָבָ֗ר לְ⁠הָשִׁיב֙ וְ⁠לִ⁠בְנ֤וֹת יְרֽוּשָׁלִַ֨ם֙

**"from the going forth of the word to return and to build Jerusalem"**

Gabriel is leaving out some of the words that in many languages a sentence would need in order to be complete. You can supply these words from the context if that would be clearer in your language. Alternate translation: \[from the time when a king issues the command to return and to build Jerusalem]

See: Ellipsis

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:25
* **Associated Articles:** Ellipsis (ID: `33267@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Daniel 9:25 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_nvmi)

מֹצָ֣א דָבָ֗ר

**"the going forth of the word"**

The implication is that **the going forth of the word** is the issuing of a royal decree. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[the issuing of the royal decree]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:25
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Daniel 9:25 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_pax7)

לְ⁠הָשִׁיב֙

**"to return"**

Here **return** is an idiom meaning “do again.” Alternate translation: \[to build Jerusalem again] or \[to rebuild Jerusalem]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:25
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Daniel 9:25 (#5) (id: en_tn_DAN_k89c)

תָּשׁוּב֙ וְ⁠נִבְנְתָה֙

**"It will return and it will be built"**

As in the instance of “return” earlier in this verse, here **It will return and it will be built** means “It will be built again.” Alternate translation: \[It will be built again] or \[It will be rebuilt]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:25
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

שִׁשִּׁ֣ים וּ⁠שְׁנַ֗יִם

**"sixty and two"**

The expression **sixty and two** describes a single number. Translate this number according to the conventions of your own language and culture. Alternate translation: \[sixty\-two]

See: Numbers

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:25
* **Associated Articles:** Numbers (ID: `33392@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Daniel 9:25 (#7) (id: en_tn_DAN_zp19)

תָּשׁוּב֙

**"It will return"**

The pronoun **It** refers to Jerusalem. It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[Jerusalem will return]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:25
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Daniel 9:25 (#8) (id: en_tn_DAN_tg7e)

וְ⁠נִבְנְתָה֙ רְח֣וֹב וְ⁠חָר֔וּץ

**"and it will be built {with} a square and a trench"**

The expression **it will be built** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[and people will build it with a square and a trench]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:25
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 9:25 (#9) (id: en_tn_DAN_odl5)

וּ⁠בְ⁠צ֖וֹק הָ⁠עִתִּֽים

**"but in distress of times"**

Gabriel is using the possessive form to describe **times** that are characterized by **distress**. If a speaker of your language would not use the possessive form for this, you could express this idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[but in distressful times]

See: Possession

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:25
* **Associated Articles:** Possession (ID: `33303@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 9:26 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_oymg)

יִכָּרֵ֥ת מָשִׁ֖יחַ

**"the anointed one will be cut off"**

The expression **will be cut off** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[enemies will cut off the anointed one]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:26
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`)

## Daniel 9:26 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_vt3j)

יִכָּרֵ֥ת מָשִׁ֖יחַ

**"the anointed one will be cut off"**

Gabriel is speaking as if the killing of the anointed one were his being **cut off**, as a branch is cut from a tree. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[the anointed one will be killed]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:26
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`)

## Daniel 9:26 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_gbsl)

וְ⁠אֵ֣ין ל֑⁠וֹ

**"and nothing {will be} to him"**

Gabriel is using this expression as a common expression of the culture to mean that the anointed one would be left with nothing. This could mean that he would have (1\) no possessions or (2\) no one to support him. If this expression does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[and he will have nothing]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:26
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`)

## Daniel 9:26 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_bgso)

וְ⁠הָ⁠עִ֨יר וְ⁠הַ⁠קֹּ֜דֶשׁ יַ֠שְׁחִית עַ֣ם נָגִ֤יד הַ⁠בָּא֙

**"And the city and the sanctuary, the people of the coming ruler will destroy"**

If it would be more natural in your language, you may want to put the information about **the people of the coming ruler** before the information about **the city and the sanctuary**. Alternate translation: \[And the people of the coming ruler will destroy the city and the sanctuary]

See: Information Structure

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:26
* **Associated Articles:** Information Structure (ID: `33286@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`)

## Daniel 9:26 (#5) (id: en_tn_DAN_ve5o)

וְ⁠קִצּ֣⁠וֹ בַ⁠שֶּׁ֔טֶף

**"And its end {will be} with a flood"**

This could mean: (1\) **its** refers to the city or (2\) it refers to the coming ruler. It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[And the end of the city will be with a flood]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 9:26
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)

**"the sacrifice and the offering"**

The terms **the sacrifice** and **the offering** mean similar things. Gabriel is using the two terms together for emphasis. If it would be clearer for your readers, you could express the emphasis with a single phrase. Alternate translation: \[all the sacrifices]

## Daniel - Chapter 10 Introduction (id: en_tn_DAN_oi0t)

Daniel 10 Introduction
======================

Structure and Formatting
------------------------

This chapter is the first part of the last section in the book, in which Daniel has a vision and man\-like figure appears to him and interprets it.

1. Narrative introduction to this section (1\)
2. Daniel relates how he had a vision and prayed for three weeks to understand it (2–3\)
3. Daniel relates how a glorious, man\-like figure appeared to him (4–9\)
4. The man explains why he was delayed (10–21\)
* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:0


## Daniel 10:1 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_xdhw)

בִּ⁠שְׁנַ֣ת שָׁל֗וֹשׁ לְ⁠כ֨וֹרֶשׁ֙ מֶ֣לֶךְ פָּרַ֔ס

**"In the year of three to Cyrus, the king of Persia"**

Daniel is using the phrase translated as **In the year of three to Cyrus, the king of Persia** to introduce a new event in the story. Use a word, phrase, or other method in your language that is natural for introducing a new event.

See: Introduction of a New Event

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:1
* **Associated Articles:** Introduction of a New Event (ID: `33419@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 10:3 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_r0tr)

לֶ֣חֶם חֲמֻד֞וֹת לֹ֣א אָכַ֗לְתִּי וּ⁠בָשָׂ֥ר וָ⁠יַ֛יִן לֹא־בָ֥א אֶל־פִּ֖⁠י וְ⁠ס֣וֹךְ לֹא־סָ֑כְתִּי

**"I ate no bread of delight, and flesh and wine did not come into my mouth, and anointing, I did not anoint {myself"**

Going without **bread of delight, and flesh and wine** and **anointing** for **three weeks** was a symbolic action by which Daniel showed that he was mourning. If it would be helpful to your readers, you could explain the significance of this action in the text of your translation or in a footnote. Alternate translation: \[To show that I was mourning, I ate no choice foods or meat or wine, and certainly, I did not anoint myself]

See: Symbolic Action

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:3
* **Associated Articles:** Symbolic Action (ID: `33402@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

לֶ֣חֶם חֲמֻד֞וֹת

**"and flesh and wine did not come into my mouth"**

Daniel is using **flesh and wine did not come into my mouth** as a common expression of the culture to mean “I did not eat meat or drink wine.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. ## Daniel 10:3 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_s4yj)

וְ⁠ס֣וֹךְ לֹא־סָ֑כְתִּי

**"and anointing, I did not anoint {myself"**

Daniel is repeating forms of the verb **anoint** in order to intensify his expression of the idea that it conveys. If your language can repeat words for intensification, it would be appropriate to do that here in your translation. If not, your language may have another way of expressing the intensification. Alternate translation: \[I did not anoint myself at all]

See: Reduplication

## Daniel 10:4 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_aj2f)

וּ⁠בְ⁠י֛וֹם עֶשְׂרִ֥ים וְ⁠אַרְבָּעָ֖ה לַ⁠חֹ֣דֶשׁ הָ⁠רִאשׁ֑וֹן

**"And on the day of twenty and four to the first month"**

Daniel is using the cardinal number **twenty and four** where your language may use an ordinal number. If your language would not represent a number in a form such as **twenty and four**, you could represent this number in a form that your language would use. Alternate translation: \[And on the twenty\-fourth day of the first month]

See: Ordinal Numbers

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:4
* **Associated Articles:** Ordinal Numbers (ID: `33393@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tigris River (ID: `place:Tigris`)

## Daniel 10:4 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_htct)

לַ⁠חֹ֣דֶשׁ הָ⁠רִאשׁ֑וֹן

**"to the first month"**

The **first** month of the Hebrew calendar includes the last part of March and the first part of April on Western calendars. You could convert the Hebrew day and month into an approximate date on the calendar that your culture uses. However, the Jews used a lunar calendar, so if you use a solar calendar, the date will be different every year, and the translation will not be entirely accurate. So you may just want to give the number of the day and the name of the month on the Hebrew calendar and say in a footnote approximately what time of year that is on your calendar.

See: Hebrew Months

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:4
* **Associated Articles:** Hebrew Months (ID: `33382@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tigris River (ID: `place:Tigris`)

וּ⁠גְוִיָּת֣⁠וֹ כְ⁠תַרְשִׁ֗ישׁ

* **Associated Articles:** Simile (ID: `33314@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 10:6 (#6) (id: en_tn_DAN_cn3h)

כְּ⁠עֵ֖ין נְחֹ֣שֶׁת קָלָ֑ל

**"were} like the eye of polished bronze"**

Here, **eye** represents the appearance or gleam of something. If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression or plain language. Alternate translation: \[were the gleam of polished bronze]

See: Metonymy

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:6
* **Associated Articles:** Metonymy (ID: `33293@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 10:6 (#7) (id: en_tn_DAN_y6p3)

וְ⁠ק֥וֹל דְּבָרָ֖י⁠ו כְּ⁠ק֥וֹל הָמֽוֹן

**"and the sound of his words {was} like the sound of a multitude"**

The point of this comparison is that just as **the sound of a multitude** is loud and roaring, so was **the sound of his words**. If it would be helpful in your language, you could make this point explicitly. Alternate translation: \[and the sound of his words was as loud and rumbling as the noise a great crowd makes]

See: Simile

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:6
* **Associated Articles:** Simile (ID: `33314@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 10:7 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_vpnh)

חֲרָדָ֤ה גְדֹלָה֙ נָפְלָ֣ה עֲלֵי⁠הֶ֔ם

**"a great trembling fell on them"**

Daniel is speaking of the terror that seized these men as if it were something that **fell on them**. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[they began to tremble violently]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:7
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Vision (ID: `keyterm:Vision`); Daniel (ID: `person:Daniel.3`)

## Daniel 10:8 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_hyc6)

וַ⁠אֲנִי֙ נִשְׁאַ֣רְתִּי לְ⁠בַדִּ֔⁠י

**"And I, I was left alone"**

Daniel is stating the pronoun **I** separately, even though the verb translated as **was left** already includes this meaning. He is doing that to emphasize that he alone remained to see the vision. If a speaker of your language would use an explicit pronoun for the same purpose, you may want to use that construction in your translation. If not, your language may have other ways of showing the meaning here. Alternate translation: \[And I myself was left alone] or \[And I myself was left entirely alone]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:8
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); Vision (ID: `keyterm:Vision`)

## Daniel 10:8 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_lrn1)

נִשְׁאַ֣רְתִּי לְ⁠בַדִּ֔⁠י

**"I was left alone"**

The expression **was left** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[And I remained alone]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:8
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); Vision (ID: `keyterm:Vision`)

## Daniel 10:8 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_yisc)

וְ⁠הוֹדִ֗⁠י נֶהְפַּ֤ךְ עָלַ⁠י֙ לְ⁠מַשְׁחִ֔ית

**"and my splendor was turned in me to destruction"**

Daniel is speaking of his appearance changing as if it **was turned** into something else. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[and my healthy appearance became deathly pale]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:8
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); Vision (ID: `keyterm:Vision`)

## Daniel 10:9 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_m8s7)

הָיִ֛יתִי נִרְדָּ֥ם עַל־פָּנַ֖⁠י

**"I was sleeping deeply upon my face"**

Daniel is using **I was sleeping deeply upon my face** as a common expression of the culture to mean “I lost consciousness and lay with my face toward the ground.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[I fell unconscious with my face down]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:9
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 10:10 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_tv2i)

**"Then behold"**

Daniel is using the term **behold** to focus his listeners’ attention on what he is about to say. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation. Alternate translation: \[Then suddenly]

See: Foreground Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:10


## Daniel 10:10 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_juye)

יָ֖ד נָ֣גְעָה בִּ֑⁠י

**"a hand touched me"**

Daniel is using one part of this being, **a hand**, to represent the whole being. If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression from your culture or state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[someone touched me]

See: Synecdoche

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:10
* **Associated Articles:** Synecdoche (ID: `33315@UWTranslationNotes`)

וַ⁠עֲמֹ֣ד עַל־עָמְדֶ֔⁠ךָ

**"Stand on your standing"**

The man is using a construction in which a verb and its object come from the same root. You may be able to use the same construction in your language to express the meaning here. Alternatively, your language may have another way of saying this. Alternate translation: \[Get to your feet]

**"for now I have been sent to you"**

אַל־תִּירָ֣א דָנִיֵּאל֒

**"May you not fear, Daniel"**

This is an imperative, but it communicates a reassurance rather than a command. If the imperative does not communicate a reassurance for your readers, then use a form in your language that communicates a reassurance. Alternate translation: \[You should not be afraid, Daniel]

נָתַ֧תָּ אֶֽת־לִבְּ⁠ךָ֛ לְ⁠הָבִ֧ין

**"you set your heart to understand"**

The man is using **you set your heart to understand** as a common expression of the culture to mean “you determined to understand.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. ## Daniel 10:12 (#5) (id: en_tn_DAN_i2rw)

וַ⁠אֲנִי־בָ֖אתִי

**"and I, I have come"**

The messenger is stating the pronoun **I** separately, even though the verb translated as **I have come** already includes this meaning. He is doing that to emphasize the importance of his role in serving God. If a speaker of your language would use an explicit pronoun for the same purpose, you may want to use that construction in your translation. If not, your language may have other ways of showing the meaning here. Alternate translation: \[and it is I who have come]

## Daniel 10:13 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_dcdv)

וְ⁠שַׂ֣ר ׀ מַלְכ֣וּת פָּרַ֗ס

**"But the prince of the kingdom of Persia"**

The implication is that **the prince of the kingdom of Persia** is not a human ruler but a spirit being who opposed the man speaking with Daniel. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[But the spirit prince who rules over the kingdom of Persia]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:13
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

**"for} twenty and one day"**

The expression **twenty and one day** means twenty\-one days. Translate this number according to the conventions of your own language and culture. Alternate translation: \[for twenty\-one days]

See: Numbers

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:13
* **Associated Articles:** Numbers (ID: `33392@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 10:13 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_czei)

מִֽיכָאֵ֗ל אַחַ֛ד הַ⁠שָּׂרִ֥ים הָ⁠רִאשֹׁנִ֖ים

**"Michael, one of the chief princes"**

The man is introducing **Michael** as a new participant in the story. Your language may have its own way of introducing new participants that you can use in your translation. Alternate translation: \[a certain one of the chief princes, named Michael]

See: Introduction of New and Old Participants

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:13
* **Associated Articles:** Introduction of New and Old Participants (ID: `33421@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 10:13 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_ts8b)

וַ⁠אֲנִי֙ נוֹתַ֣רְתִּי שָׁ֔ם אֵ֖צֶל מַלְכֵ֥י פָרָֽס

**"for I, I had been left there beside the kings of Persia"**

The expression **had been left** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[for I, I remained there beside the kings of Persia]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:13

## Daniel 10:13 (#5) (id: en_tn_DAN_ezn2)

מַלְכֵ֥י פָרָֽס

**"the kings of Persia"**

The standard written Hebrew text as we have it today reads “the kings of Persia.” The ULT follows that reading. Other ancient manuscripts read “the prince of the kings of Persia.” If a translation of the Bible exists in your region, you may wish to use the reading that it uses. If a translation of the Bible does not exist in your region, you may wish to use the reading of the ULT.

See: Textual Variants

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:13
* **Associated Articles:** Textual Variants (ID: `33405@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Michael (ID: `person:Michael.11`); Persia (ID: `place:Persia`)

בְּ⁠אַחֲרִ֣ית הַ⁠יָּמִ֑ים

**"at the end of days"**

The man is using **at the end of days** as a common expression of the culture to mean “in the distant future.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. ע֥וֹד \& לַ⁠יָּמִֽים

**"yet for days"**

Daniel is using **yet for days** as a common expression of the culture to mean “sometime far in the future.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. **"And behold"**

כִּ⁠דְמוּת֙ בְּנֵ֣י אָדָ֔ם

**"one} according to the likeness of the sons of man"**

Daniel is using **the sons of man** as a common expression of the culture to mean “a human being.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. ## Daniel 10:16 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_imhs)

נֶהֶפְכ֤וּ צִירַ⁠י֙ עָלַ֔⁠י

**"my pains have turned onto me"**

Daniel is speaking of being overcome with anguish as if his pains **have turned onto** him. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[I have become full of anguish]

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:16
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 10:17 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_flxk)

וְ⁠הֵ֣יךְ יוּכַ֗ל עֶ֤בֶד אֲדֹנִ⁠י֙ זֶ֔ה לְ⁠דַבֵּ֖ר עִם־אֲדֹ֣נִ⁠י זֶ֑ה

**"And how shall this servant of my lord be able to speak with this my lord"**

Daniel is using the question form to say emphatically that he is not able to speak with the one who is addressing him. If a speaker of your language would not use the question form for that purpose, you could translate this as a statement or as an exclamation. Alternate translation: \[And this servant of my lord is certainly not able to speak with this my lord!]

See: Rhetorical Question

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:17
* **Associated Articles:** Rhetorical Question (ID: `33310@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`)

## Daniel 10:17 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_hxka)

וְ⁠הֵ֣יךְ יוּכַ֗ל עֶ֤בֶד אֲדֹנִ⁠י֙ זֶ֔ה לְ⁠דַבֵּ֖ר עִם־אֲדֹ֣נִ⁠י זֶ֑ה

**"And how shall this servant of my lord be able to speak with this my lord"**

Even though Daniel is addressing this being directly, he is speaking of him in the third person as **this my lord**, and he is speaking of himself in the third person as **this servant**, in order to show respect. If it would be helpful in your language, you could translate this in respectful forms in the first and second person. Alternate translation: \[And how shall I, your servant, be able to speak with you, my lord]

See: First, Second or Third Person

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:17
* **Associated Articles:** First, Second or Third Person (ID: `33256@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`)

## Daniel 10:17 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_vy5l)

וּ⁠נְשָׁמָ֖ה לֹ֥א נִשְׁאֲרָה־בִֽ⁠י

**"and breath is not left in me"**

The expression **is not left** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[and no breath remains in me]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:17
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`)

## Daniel 10:17 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_sj8t)

וּ⁠נְשָׁמָ֖ה לֹ֥א נִשְׁאֲרָה־בִֽ⁠י

**"and breath is not left in me"**

Daniel says **breath is not left in me** as an extreme statement for emphasis, since he is still breathing and speaking. If it would be helpful in your language, you could use a different way to express the emphasis. Alternate translation: \[and I can barely breathe]

See: Hyperbole

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:17
* **Associated Articles:** Hyperbole (ID: `33281@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`)

## Daniel 10:18 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_s2id)

וַ⁠יֹּ֧סֶף וַ⁠יִּגַּע־בִּ֛⁠י כְּ⁠מַרְאֵ֥ה אָדָ֖ם

**"And {the one} according to the appearance of a man added and touched me"**

The phrase **added and touched** expresses a single idea. The word **added** tells more about **touched**. If it would be more natural in your language, you could express this meaning in a different way. Alternate translation: \[And the one who looked like a man touched me again]

See: Hendiadys

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 10:18
* **Associated Articles:** Hendiadys (ID: `33280@UWTranslationNotes`)

The man is repeating forms of the verb **be strong** in order to intensify his expression of the idea that it conveys. If your language can repeat words for intensification, it would be appropriate to do that here in your translation. If not, your language may have another way of expressing the intensification. Alternate translation: \[Be strong indeed]

## Daniel 10:20 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_f4at)

**"and behold"**

**"And there is not one being strong with me against these"**

## Daniel - Chapter 11 Introduction (id: en_tn_DAN_djfo)

Daniel 11 Introduction
======================

Structure and Formatting
------------------------

This chapter continues the last section in the book, in which Daniel has a vision and man\-like figure appears to him and interprets it.

1. The man finishes his explanation of why he was delayed (1\)
2. The man begins to explain the meaning of Daniel’s vision (2–45\)

    1. Wars between the kings of the north and south (2–20\)
        2. An evil king of the north will cause great trouble for the Jews (21–35\)
        3. This king of the north will become powerful but ultimately be destroyed (36–45\)

Translation issues in this chapter
----------------------------------

### The identification of the kings of the north and south

As in chapter 8, the person who interprets Daniel’s vision does specify what some of the figures in it represent. He speaks of Persian kings and their wars against “the kingdom of Javan,” that is, Greece (11:2\). He then describes how “a mighty king will stand” (11:3\), presumably Alexander the Great of Greece, and how “his kingdom will be broken and divided toward the four winds of the heavens, but not to his posterity,” just as Alexander’s empire was divided among four of his generals after he died. This leads many interpreters to believe that the kings of the north are the kings who ruled the northern part of what had been that empire and that the kings of the south are the ones who ruled the southern part, which was based in Egypt. (In 11:7–8, the man even describes how one king of the south will defeat the king of the north and bring their gods “into captivity to Egypt.”) However, even if the accounts of the kings of the north and south are initially describing the actual struggle for power between two Greek empires that succeeded Alexander’s, it is not clear at what point the vision might shift from historical events subsequent to Daniel but prior to the present day to future events that will take place “at the time of the end” (11:40\). So as in the case of the other visions in the book, we recommend that translators not add any identifying information beyond what the book specifically says, either in the text or in footnotes.

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:0

## Daniel 11:4 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_h9iv)

וּ⁠כְ⁠עָמְד⁠וֹ֙ תִּשָּׁבֵ֣ר מַלְכוּת֔⁠וֹ וְ⁠תֵחָ֕ץ לְ⁠אַרְבַּ֖ע רוּח֣וֹת הַ⁠שָּׁמָ֑יִם וְ⁠לֹ֣א לְ⁠אַחֲרִית֗⁠וֹ וְ⁠לֹ֤א כְ⁠מָשְׁל⁠וֹ֙ אֲשֶׁ֣ר מָשָׁ֔ל כִּ֤י תִנָּתֵשׁ֙ מַלְכוּת֔⁠וֹ וְ⁠לַ⁠אֲחֵרִ֖ים מִ⁠לְּ⁠בַד־אֵֽלֶּה

**"But as he stands, his kingdom will be broken and divided toward the four winds of the heavens, but not to his posterity, and not according to his dominion {by} which he ruled. For his kingdom will be uprooted, and to others besides these"**

The expression **as he stands** indicates that **his kingdom will be broken and divided** while this king is still ruling. If it would be helpful to your readers, you could describe the time relationship more explicitly. Alternate translation: \[But at the same time that he is still reigning]

See: Connect — Simultaneous Time Relationship

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:4
* **Associated Articles:** Connect — Simultaneous Time Relationship (ID: `33334@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 11:6 (#5) (id: en_tn_DAN_k2cs)

וְ⁠תִנָּתֵ֨ן

**"And she will be given up"**

The expression **will be given up** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[And enemies will hand her over]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:6
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); Fairness (ID: `keyterm:Fairness`)

## Daniel 11:6 (#6) (id: en_tn_DAN_cw6u)

וְ⁠הַ⁠יֹּ֣לְדָ֔⁠הּ

**"and the one having fathered her"**

The expression translated as **and the one having fathered her** represents the standard written Hebrew text as we have it today. This reading, however, does not seem to make sense. Scholars have proposed that a scribe may have added the wrong vowels to the original consonants of the text, resulting in this expression. With different vowels, it would say “her child,” which seems to fit the meaning of this passage better. The UST follows that interpretation. You may want to follow the major translation in your area. Alternate translation: \[and her child]

See: Alternative Readings

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); Fairness (ID: `keyterm:Fairness`)

## Daniel 11:7 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_lv10)

וְ⁠עָמַ֛ד מִ⁠נֵּ֥צֶר שָׁרָשֶׁ֖י⁠הָ כַּנּ֑⁠וֹ

**"But from a branch of her roots, one will stand {in} his place"**

The angel is using this phrase as a common expression of the culture to mean “he will rule as king after him.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[But from a branch of her roots, one will rule as king after him]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:7
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fortress (ID: `realia:Fortress`)

## Daniel 11:7 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_qeyj)

מִ⁠נֵּ֥צֶר שָׁרָשֶׁ֖י⁠הָ

**"from a branch of her roots, one"**

Here the angel is speaking as if the family of this woman were a plant with **roots** from which **a branch** would grow. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[from among her family members, one]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:7
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fortress (ID: `realia:Fortress`)

## Daniel 11:7 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_x7zc)

וְ⁠עָשָׂ֥ה בָ⁠הֶ֖ם וְ⁠הֶחֱזִֽיק

**"And he will do with them and be strong"**

The phrase **do with them and be strong** expresses a single idea. The word **strong** tells more about **do**. If it would be more natural in your language, you could express this meaning in a different way. Alternate translation: \[he will act powerfully against them] or \[he will subjugate them]

See: Hendiadys

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:7
* **Associated Articles:** Hendiadys (ID: `33280@UWTranslationNotes`)

**"he will bring into captivity {to} Egypt"**

If your language does not use an abstract noun for the idea of **captivity**, you could express the same idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[he will capture and carry away to Egypt]

**"the silver and gold vessels of their delight"**

The angel is using the possessive form to describe **vessels** that are characterized by being objects of **their delight**. The expression **will be stirred up** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[And his sons will stir themselves up for battle]

## Daniel 11:10 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_pi33)

The angel is repeating forms of the verb **come** in order to intensify his expression of the idea that it conveys. If your language can repeat words for intensification, it would be appropriate to do that here in your translation. If not, your language may have another way of expressing the intensification. Alternate translation: \[And it will surely come]

## Daniel 11:11 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_lih2)

וְ⁠הֶעֱמִיד֙ הָמ֣וֹן רָ֔ב וְ⁠נִתַּ֥ן הֶ⁠הָמ֖וֹן בְּ⁠יָדֽ⁠וֹ

**"And he will make a great multitude stand, but the multitude will be given into his hand"**

The pronoun **he** refers to the king of the north, while the pronoun **his** refers to the king of the south. It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[And the king of the north will make a great multitude stand, but the multitude will be given into the hand of the king of the south]

* **Associated Articles:** Ordinal Numbers (ID: `33393@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 11:14 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_e9c8)

* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:17 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_p098)

וְ⁠יָשֵׂ֣ם ׀ פָּ֠נָי⁠ו לָ⁠ב֞וֹא

**"And he will set his face to come"**

The angel is using **he will set his face** as a common expression of the culture to mean “he will firmly decide.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[And he will firmly decide to come]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:17
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:17 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_o3cz)

וִ⁠ישָׁרִ֥ים עִמּ֖⁠וֹ וְ⁠עָשָׂ֑ה

**"and uprightnesses with him, thus he will do"**

The angel is leaving out some of the words that in many languages a sentence would need in order to be complete. You can supply these words from the context if that would be clearer in your language. Alternate translation: \[and he will bring terms of agreement with him, and thus he will act]

See: Ellipsis

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:17
* **Associated Articles:** Ellipsis (ID: `33267@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:17 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_nxef)

וּ⁠בַ֤ת הַ⁠נָּשִׁים֙ יִתֶּן־ל֣⁠וֹ לְ⁠הַשְׁחִיתָ֔⁠הּ

**"And he will give a daughter of women to him, to ruin it"**

The pronoun **he** refers to the king of the north, while the pronoun **him** refers to the king of the south. The pronoun **it** refers to the kingdom of the south It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[And the king of the north will give a daughter of women to the king of the south, to ruin the kingdom of the south]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:17
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:17 (#5) (id: en_tn_DAN_fd8w)

וּ⁠בַ֤ת הַ⁠נָּשִׁים֙

**"a daughter of women"**

The angel is using **a daughter of women** as a common expression of the culture to mean “a young woman.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[a young woman]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:17
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:17 (#6) (id: en_tn_DAN_zy48)

לְ⁠הַשְׁחִיתָ֔⁠הּ

**"to ruin it"**

The pronoun **it** refers either to (1\) the kingdom of the south, which this king wants to destroy or (2\) the daughter herself, whom he is willing to use for his own purposes. It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[to ruin the southern kingdom]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:17
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:17 (#7) (id: en_tn_DAN_oiaz)

וְ⁠לֹ֥א תַעֲמֹ֖ד וְ⁠לֹא־ל֥⁠וֹ תִהְיֶֽה

**"But it will not stand, and it will not be for him"**

These two clauses mean basically the same thing. The second emphasizes the meaning of the first by repeating the same idea with different words. If it would be helpful to your readers, you could combine them and express the emphasis in another way. Alternate translation: \[But this plan will not succeed and will not benefit him at all]

See: Parallelism

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:17
* **Associated Articles:** Parallelism (ID: `33299@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:18 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_okoz)

וְ⁠הִשְׁבִּ֨ית קָצִ֤ין חֶרְפָּת⁠וֹ֙ ל֔⁠וֹ

**"But a commander will end his reproach {that is} to him"**

If your language does not use an abstract noun for the idea of **reproach**, you could express the same idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[But a commander will stop him from insulting others]

See: Abstract Nouns

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:18
* **Associated Articles:** Abstract Nouns (ID: `33257@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:18 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_enu2)

חֶרְפָּת⁠וֹ֙ ל֔⁠וֹ

**"his reproach {that is} to him"**

The pronouns **his** and **him** both refer to the king of the north. It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[the king of the north’s own reproach]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:18
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:18 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_futd)

חֶרְפָּת⁠וֹ֙ ל֔⁠וֹ

**"his reproach {that is} to him"**

It might seem that the expression **his reproach that is to him** contains extra information that would not be natural to express in your language. If so, you could shorten it. Alternate translation: \[his reproach]

See: Making Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information Explicit

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:18
* **Associated Articles:** Making Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information Explicit (ID: `33274@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:18 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_ws9j)

בִּלְתִּ֥י חֶרְפָּת֖⁠וֹ יָשִׁ֥יב

**"without his {own} reproach, he will return {it"**

The pronouns “his” and “he” refer to the “commander”; “him” refers to the king of the north.

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:18
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:19 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_waaj)

וְ⁠נִכְשַׁ֥ל וְ⁠נָפַ֖ל וְ⁠לֹ֥א יִמָּצֵֽא

**"but he will stumble and fall, and he will not be found"**

The angel is referring to the death of this king in a polite way by using the phrase **he will stumble and fall, and he will not be found**. Depending on what is helpful for your readers, you could use a polite way of referring to this in your language, or you could state this plainly. Alternate translation: \[but he will die there and disappear from history]

See: Euphemism

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:19
* **Associated Articles:** Euphemism (ID: `33268@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fortress (ID: `realia:Fortress`)

## Daniel 11:19 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_qmul)

וְ⁠לֹ֥א יִמָּצֵֽא

**"and he will not be found"**

The expression **will not be found** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[and no one will find him]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:19
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fortress (ID: `realia:Fortress`)

## Daniel 11:20 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_ngl8)

וְ⁠עָמַ֧ד עַל־כַּנּ֛⁠וֹ מַעֲבִ֥יר נוֹגֵ֖שׂ הֶ֣דֶר מַלְכ֑וּת

**"And one making an exactor pass through the splendor of the kingdom will stand in his place"**

The implication is that this king will send a tax collector throughout his realm to take money from the people. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[And a king who sends a tax collector throughout the glorious realm to take money from the people will rule]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:20
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:20 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_mib2)

הֶ֣דֶר מַלְכ֑וּת

**"the splendor of the kingdom"**

If your language does not use an abstract noun for the idea of **the splendor**, you could express the same idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[the splendid kingdom]

See: Abstract Nouns

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:20
* **Associated Articles:** Abstract Nouns (ID: `33257@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:20 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_u5py)

יִשָּׁבֵ֔ר

**"he will be broken"**

The expression **will be broken** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[someone will break him]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:20
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:20 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_nxjp)

יִשָּׁבֵ֔ר

**"he will be broken"**

Here the angel is speaking as if this king were an object that would be **broken**, meaning that he will die. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[he will die]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:20
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:20 (#5) (id: en_tn_DAN_opnc)

וְ⁠לֹ֥א בְ⁠אַפַּ֖יִם וְ⁠לֹ֥א בְ⁠מִלְחָמָֽה

**"but not in nostrils and not in battle"**

The angel is leaving out some of the words that in many languages a sentence would need in order to be complete. You can supply these words from the context if that would be clearer in your language. Alternate translation: \[but he will not be broken in anger and he will not be broken in battle]

See: Ellipsis

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:20
* **Associated Articles:** Ellipsis (ID: `33267@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:20 (#6) (id: en_tn_DAN_adz3)

וְ⁠לֹ֥א בְ⁠אַפַּ֖יִם

**"but not in nostrils"**

The angel is using **in nostrils** as a common expression of the culture to mean “in anger.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[but not in anger]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:20
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:21 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_bcah)

וְ⁠עָמַ֤ד עַל־כַּנּ⁠וֹ֙ נִבְזֶ֔ה

**"And a despised one will stand in his place"**

The expression **a despised one** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[And a man whom people despise will stand in his place]

* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 11:21 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_qgsz)

וְ⁠לֹא־נָתְנ֥וּ עָלָ֖י⁠ו ה֣וֹד מַלְכ֑וּת

**"and they will not give the splendor of the kingdom to him"**

The pronoun **they** does not refer to any specific people. The angel is using it to mean people in general. It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[and people will not give the splendor of the kingdom to him]

## Daniel 11:21 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_xv5k)

וּ⁠בָ֣א בְ⁠שַׁלְוָ֔ה

**"And he will come in security"**

The angel is using **he will come in security** as a common expression of the culture to mean “he will come when people feel safe and are not expecting an attack.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[And he will come when people feel safe and unsuspecting]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:21
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`)

## Daniel 11:22 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_r7r6)

וּ⁠זְרֹע֥וֹת הַ⁠שֶּׁ֛טֶף יִשָּׁטְפ֥וּ

**"And the arms of the flood will be swept away"**

Here the angel is speaking as if an army were a flood; speaking as if a flood had arms; speaking as if a flood could be swept away (rather than sweeping things away itself). Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[And the vast armies will be utterly defeated]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:22
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`)

## Daniel 11:22 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_ou4f)

וּ⁠זְרֹע֥וֹת הַ⁠שֶּׁ֛טֶף יִשָּׁטְפ֥וּ

**"And the arms of the flood will be swept away"**

The expression **will be swept away** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[And he will sweep away the vast armies]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:22
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`)

## Daniel 11:22 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_v5en)

וּ⁠זְרֹע֥וֹת הַ⁠שֶּׁ֛טֶף יִשָּׁטְפ֥וּ

**"And the arms of the flood will be swept away"**

The noun “flood” and the verb “swept away” are from the same root. Alternate translation: \[And the arms of the sweeping away will be swept away] or \[And the arms of the flood will be flooded away]

See: Poetry

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:22
* **Associated Articles:** Poetry (ID: `33422@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`)

מִ⁠לְּ⁠פָנָ֖י⁠ו

**"from to his face"**

וְ⁠יִשָּׁבֵ֑רוּ

**"and they will be broken"**

וְ⁠יִשָּׁבֵ֑רוּ

**"and they will be broken"**

וְ⁠גַ֖ם נְגִ֥יד בְּרִֽית

**"and also the leader of the covenant"**

The angel is leaving out some of the words that in many languages a sentence would need in order to be complete. You can supply these words from the context if that would be clearer in your language. Alternate translation: \[and also the leader of the covenant will be swept away and broken]

## Daniel 11:22 (#9) (id: en_tn_DAN_ij96)

נְגִ֥יד בְּרִֽית

**"the leader of the covenant"**

The implication is that **the leader of the covenant** is the high priest, who leads the people in keeping the covenant that God made with them. ## Daniel 11:23 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_ddl9)

וּ⁠מִן־הִֽתְחַבְּר֥וּת אֵלָ֖י⁠ו יַעֲשֶׂ֣ה מִרְמָ֑ה וְ⁠עָלָ֥ה וְ⁠עָצַ֖ם בִּ⁠מְעַט־גּֽוֹי

**"And after the joining together with him, he will act {with} deceit. And he will go up, and he will become strong with a few of people"**

The expression **after the joining together with him** indicates that these events will happen after other rulers make an alliance with this king. Your language may have a connecting phrase that you can use to show this. Alternate translation: \[Once others have made an alliance with him, he will act with deceit. Then he will go up, and he will become strong with a few people]

See: Connect — Sequential Time Relationship

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:23
* **Associated Articles:** Connect — Sequential Time Relationship (ID: `33333@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Deceit (ID: `keyterm:Deceit`)

## Daniel 11:23 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_sh3f)

וּ⁠מִן־הִֽתְחַבְּר֥וּת אֵלָ֖י⁠ו

**"And after the joining together with him"**

This seems to refer to lesser kings or groups among peoples he wanted to control joining with this “king of the north.”

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:23
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Deceit (ID: `keyterm:Deceit`)

## Daniel 11:23 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_beot)

יַעֲשֶׂ֣ה מִרְמָ֑ה

**"he will act {with} deceit"**

If your language does not use an abstract noun for the idea of **deceit**, you could express the same idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[he will act in a deceitful way]

See: Abstract Nouns

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:23
* **Associated Articles:** Abstract Nouns (ID: `33257@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Deceit (ID: `keyterm:Deceit`)

## Daniel 11:24 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_jve7)

וּ⁠בְ⁠מִשְׁמַנֵּ֣י מְדִינָה֮

**"even into the fatnesses of the province"**

Here the angel is speaking as if the richest parts of the province were its **fatnesses**. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[even into the richest parts of the province]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:24
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:24 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_p6qh)

אֲבֹתָי⁠ו֙ וַ⁠אֲב֣וֹת אֲבֹתָ֔י⁠ו

**"his fathers and the fathers of his fathers"**

Here the angel is speaking as if the ancestors of this king were his **fathers**. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[his ancestors and their ancestors before them]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:24
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:24 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_jd29)

וּ⁠בְ⁠מִשְׁמַנֵּ֣י \& בִּזָּ֧ה וְ⁠שָׁלָ֛ל וּ⁠רְכ֖וּשׁ לָ⁠הֶ֣ם יִבְז֑וֹר

**"even into the fatnesses of \& He will scatter plunder and spoil and wealth to them"**

The pronoun **He** refers to the king, while the pronoun **them** refers to his soldiers and followers. It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[even into the fatnesses of the province, the king will scatter plunder and spoil and wealth to his soldiers and followers]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:24
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:24 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_x10c)

בִּזָּ֧ה וְ⁠שָׁלָ֛ל וּ⁠רְכ֖וּשׁ

**"plunder and spoil and wealth"**

The terms **plunder**, **spoil**, and **wealth** mean similar things. The angel is using the three terms together for emphasis. If it would be clearer for your readers, you could express the emphasis with a single phrase. Alternate translation: \[great amounts of captured goods]

See: Doublet

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:24
* **Associated Articles:** Doublet (ID: `33266@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:24 (#5) (id: en_tn_DAN_ni6o)

יְחַשֵּׁ֥ב מַחְשְׁבֹתָ֖י⁠ו

**"and he will devise his devices"**

The angel is using a construction in which a verb and its object come from the same root. You may be able to use the same construction in your language to express the meaning here. Alternatively, your language may have another way of saying this.

See: Poetry

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:24
* **Associated Articles:** Poetry (ID: `33422@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:24 (#6) (id: en_tn_DAN_xm1h)

וְ⁠עַד־עֵֽת

**"but until a time"**

The angel is leaving out some of the words that in many languages a sentence would need in order to be complete. You can supply these words from the context if that would be clearer in your language. Alternate translation: \[but only until a certain time has passed]

See: Ellipsis

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:24
* **Associated Articles:** Ellipsis (ID: `33267@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:25 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_afjw)

וְ⁠יָעֵר֩ כֹּח֨⁠וֹ וּ⁠לְבָב֜⁠וֹ

**"And he will awaken his strength and his heart"**

The angel is using **he will awaken his strength and his heart** as a common expression of the culture to mean “he will summon all of his power and courage.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[And he will summon all of his power and courage]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:25
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`)

## Daniel 11:25 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_twt6)

וּ⁠מֶ֣לֶךְ הַ⁠נֶּ֗גֶב יִתְגָּרֶה֙ לַ⁠מִּלְחָמָ֔ה

**"And the king of the south will be stirred up to battle"**

The expression **will be stirred up** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[And the king of the south will rouse himself to battle]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:25
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`)

## Daniel 11:25 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_jw5r)

בְּ⁠חַֽיִל־גָּד֥וֹל וְ⁠עָצ֖וּם עַד־מְאֹ֑ד

**"with an army great and mighty unto much"**

The terms **great** and **mighty** mean similar things. The angel is using the two terms together for emphasis. If it would be clearer for your readers, you could express the emphasis with a single phrase. Alternate translation: \[with an extremely powerful army]

See: Doublet

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:25
* **Associated Articles:** Doublet (ID: `33266@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`)

## Daniel 11:25 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_sm2n)

כִּֽי־יַחְשְׁב֥וּ עָלָ֖י⁠ו מַחֲשָׁבֽוֹת

**"for they will devise devices against him"**

The pronoun **they** refers to officials of the king of the south who will betray him. It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[for his own officials will devise devices against him]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:25
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`)

## Daniel 11:26 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_os6c)

וְ⁠אֹכְלֵ֧י פַת־בָּג֛⁠וֹ

**"Even the eaters of his portion"**

The angel is using **the eaters of his portion** as a common expression of the culture to mean “the men who eat at his table as his trusted advisers.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[Even those who share his food as his trusted friends]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:26
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:26 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_j9nz)

וְ⁠חֵיל֣⁠וֹ יִשְׁט֑וֹף

**"And his army will be swept away"**

The expression **will be swept away** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[And his army will collapse]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:26
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:26 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_rumd)

וְ⁠נָפְל֖וּ חֲלָלִ֥ים רַבִּֽים

**"and many will fall slain"**

The expression **slain** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[and many will fall as enemies kill them]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:26
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:27 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_k5kg)

לְבָבָ֣⁠ם לְ⁠מֵרָ֔ע

**"their heart {will be} to evil"**

Here the angel is speaking as if the intentions of these two kings were their **heart**. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[their intentions will be evil]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:27
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:27 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_p3je)

וְ⁠עַל־שֻׁלְחָ֥ן אֶחָ֖ד כָּזָ֣ב יְדַבֵּ֑רוּ

**"and at one table they will speak falsehood"**

Here, **one table** represents “the negotiations between them.” If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression or plain language. Alternate translation: \[and while negotiating together, they will speak falsehood]

See: Metonymy

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:27
* **Associated Articles:** Metonymy (ID: `33293@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:27 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_euq7)

כָּזָ֣ב יְדַבֵּ֑רוּ

**"they will speak falsehood"**

If your language does not use an abstract noun for the idea of **falsehood**, you could express the same idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[they will speak deceitfully to each other]

See: Abstract Nouns

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:27
* **Associated Articles:** Abstract Nouns (ID: `33257@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:27 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_ukxa)

וְ⁠לֹ֣א תִצְלָ֔ח

**"But it will not prosper"**

The pronoun **it** refers to the scheming of these two kings. It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[But their scheming will not prosper]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:27
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:27 (#5) (id: en_tn_DAN_fpup)

כִּי־ע֥וֹד קֵ֖ץ לַ⁠מּוֹעֵֽד

**"for the end {is} still for the appointed time"**

The implication is that God has determined the time when these things will come to an end. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[for the end will only come at the time that God has appointed]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:27
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:28 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_luoh)

בְּרִ֣ית קֹ֑דֶשׁ

**"the covenant of holiness"**

The angel is using the possessive form to describe a **covenant** that is characterized by **holiness**. If a speaker of your language would not use the possessive form for this, you could express this idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[the holy covenant]

See: Possession

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:28
* **Associated Articles:** Possession (ID: `33303@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`)

## Daniel 11:28 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_h0kn)

וְ⁠עָשָׂ֖ה

**"And he will act"**

The implication is that this king will act against the people who belong to the covenant. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[And he will take action against the people of the covenant]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:28
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`)

## Daniel 11:29 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_n95g)

וְ⁠לֹֽא־תִהְיֶ֥ה כָ⁠רִאשֹׁנָ֖ה וְ⁠כָ⁠אַחֲרֹנָֽה

**"But it will not be like the former and like the latter"**

The angel is using **But it will not be like the former and like the latter** as a common expression of the culture to mean “this time will not turn out like the previous times.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[But history will not repeat itself this time] or \[But this campaign will not go the way the earlier ones did]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:29
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:30 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_j1dt)

כִּתִּים֙

**"Kittim"**

The word **Kittim** is the name of a place.

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:30
* **Associated Articles:** How to Translate Names (ID: `33391@UWTranslationNotes`)
