## Daniel 11:30 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_yfdm)

וְ⁠יָבֵ֔ן עַל־עֹזְבֵ֖י בְּרִ֥ית קֹֽדֶשׁ

**"and he will attend to the forsakers of the covenant of holiness"**

The angel is using **he will attend to** as a common expression of the culture to mean “he will show favor to.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[and he will show favor to those people who forsake the covenant of holiness]

הַ⁠מִּקְדָּ֤שׁ הַ⁠מָּעוֹז֙

**"the sanctuary, the fortress"**

The implication is that **the sanctuary** is the temple in Jerusalem. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[the temple in Jerusalem, that fortified sanctuary]

## Daniel 11:31 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_aa38)

הַ⁠תָּמִ֔יד

**"the perpetual sacrifice"**

The implication is that **the perpetual sacrifice** is the offering that the priests presented every morning and every evening. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[the sacrifice that the priests offered morning and evening without fail]

## Daniel 11:31 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_gnq1)

הַ⁠שִּׁקּ֥וּץ מְשׁוֹמֵֽם

**"the abomination making desolate"**

The implication is that **the abomination making desolate** is an idolatrous object that these men will set up in the temple, which will make the temple unfit for the worship of God. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[an idolatrous object in the temple that will make it unfit for worshiping God]

* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 11:32 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_qt5j)

וּ⁠מַרְשִׁיעֵ֣י בְרִ֔ית יַחֲנִ֖יף בַּ⁠חֲלַקּ֑וֹת

**"And he will pollute with flatteries the wicked actors of the covenant"**

If it would be more natural in your language, you may want to put the information about **the wicked actors of the covenant** before the information about **flatteries**. Alternate translation: \[And the people who disobey the covenant, he will pollute with flatteries]

See: Information Structure

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:32
* **Associated Articles:** Information Structure (ID: `33286@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); To Act Wickedly (ID: `keyterm:ActWickedly`)

## Daniel 11:32 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_d417)

יַחֲנִ֖יף בַּ⁠חֲלַקּ֑וֹת

**"And he will pollute with flatteries"**

Here the angel is speaking as if this king would **pollute** these people, meaning that he will lead them into wickedness. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[And he will use flattery to corrupt]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:32
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); To Act Wickedly (ID: `keyterm:ActWickedly`)

## Daniel 11:32 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_aoyh)

וְ⁠עַ֛ם יֹדְעֵ֥י אֱלֹהָ֖י⁠ו

**"But the people knowing its God"**

In this verse, the word **people** is singular in form, but it refers to all of the Israelites as a group. That is why the angel uses the singular pronoun **its**. If it would be helpful in your language, you could use plural forms here. Alternate translation: \[But the people who know their God]

See: Collective Nouns

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:32
* **Associated Articles:** Collective Nouns (ID: `33324@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); To Act Wickedly (ID: `keyterm:ActWickedly`)

## Daniel 11:32 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_hzp5)

וְ⁠עַ֛ם יֹדְעֵ֥י אֱלֹהָ֖י⁠ו

**"But the people knowing its God"**

The angel is using **knowing its God** as a common expression of the culture to mean “remaining faithful to its God.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[But the people remaining faithful to its God]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:32
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); To Act Wickedly (ID: `keyterm:ActWickedly`)

## Daniel 11:32 (#5) (id: en_tn_DAN_gph6)

אֱלֹהָ֖י⁠ו

**"its God"**

The angel is not using this possessive form **its God** to indicate that these people possess or control God. Rather, the angel is using these words to indicate that God is the one whom these people worship. Alternate translation: \[the God they worship]

See: Possession

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:32
* **Associated Articles:** Possession (ID: `33303@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); To Act Wickedly (ID: `keyterm:ActWickedly`)

## Daniel 11:32 (#6) (id: en_tn_DAN_tfmf)

יַחֲזִ֥קוּ וְ⁠עָשֽׂוּ

**"they will be strong and act"**

The phrase **be strong and act** expresses a single idea. The word **be strong** tells more about **act**. If it would be more natural in your language, you could express this meaning in a different way. Alternate translation: \[they will act strongly]

See: Hendiadys

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:32
* **Associated Articles:** Hendiadys (ID: `33280@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 11:33 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_pols)

וּ⁠מַשְׂכִּ֣ילֵי עָ֔ם

**"And the makers wise of the people"**

By **the makers wise of the people**, the angel means the wise leaders among the Jewish people who will teach and encourage others to remain faithful to God despite the persecution. You could indicate this explicitly in your translation if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[And the wise leaders among the Jewish people]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:33
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 11:33 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_wqee)

וּ⁠מַשְׂכִּ֣ילֵי עָ֔ם יָבִ֖ינוּ לָֽ⁠רַבִּ֑ים

**"And the makers wise of the people will make many understand"**

This is poetic language. If your language has a way to indicate poetry, you could use it here. Alternate translation: \[And those who teach to make others wise will lead many to understand]

See: Poetry

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:33
* **Associated Articles:** Poetry (ID: `33422@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Daniel 11:33 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_bja8)

וְ⁠נִכְשְׁל֞וּ

**"But they will stumble"**

The angel is referring to dying in a polite way by using the phrase **they will stumble**. Depending on what is helpful for your readers, you could use a polite way of referring to this in your language, or you could state this plainly. Alternate translation: \[they will perish] or \[they will die]

See: Euphemism

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:33
* **Associated Articles:** Euphemism (ID: `33268@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 11:33 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_wgfn)

בְּ⁠חֶ֧רֶב וּ⁠בְ⁠לֶהָבָ֛ה

**"by the sword and by flame"**

Here, **the sword** and **flame** represent “violent death.” If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression or plain language. Alternate translation: \[by violent death]

See: Metonymy

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:33
* **Associated Articles:** Metonymy (ID: `33293@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Daniel 11:33 (#5) (id: en_tn_DAN_dkuf)

יָמִֽים

**"for} days"**

Here, **days** represents “a period of time.” If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression or plain language. Alternate translation: \[for a length of time]

See: Metonymy

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:33
* **Associated Articles:** Metonymy (ID: `33293@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Daniel 11:34 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_rdkm)

וּ⁠בְ⁠הִכָּ֣שְׁלָ֔⁠ם יֵעָזְר֖וּ עֵ֣זֶר מְעָ֑ט וְ⁠נִלְו֧וּ עֲלֵי⁠הֶ֛ם רַבִּ֖ים בַּ⁠חֲלַקְלַקּֽוֹת

**"And when they stumble, they will be helped {with} a little help. And many will join themselves with them by intrigues"**

The expression **when they stumble** indicates that these people **will be helped** at the same time as when they are suffering in this way. If it would be helpful to your readers, you could describe the time relationship more explicitly. Alternate translation: \[And during the time when they are stumbling, they will be helped with a little help. And many will join themselves with them by intrigues]

See: Connect — Simultaneous Time Relationship

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:34
* **Associated Articles:** Connect — Simultaneous Time Relationship (ID: `33334@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:34 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_g060)

יֵעָזְר֖וּ עֵ֣זֶר מְעָ֑ט

**"they will be helped {with} a little help"**

The expression **will be helped** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[others will give them a little help]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:34
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:34 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_vg8p)

וְ⁠נִלְו֧וּ עֲלֵי⁠הֶ֛ם רַבִּ֖ים בַּ⁠חֲלַקְלַקּֽוֹת

**"And many will join themselves with them by intrigues"**

The angel is using **will join themselves with them by intrigues** as a common expression of the culture to mean “will pretend to support them without being sincere.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[And many will pretend to support them without being sincere]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:34
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 11:35 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_rzrg)

וּ⁠מִן־הַ⁠מַּשְׂכִּילִ֣ים יִכָּֽשְׁל֗וּ

**"And some of the ones making wise will stumble"**

The angel is referring to the death of these people in a polite way by using the phrase **will stumble**. Depending on what is helpful for your readers, you could use a polite way of referring to this in your language, or you could state this plainly. Alternate translation: \[And some of the ones teaching others will lose their lives]

See: Euphemism

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:35
* **Associated Articles:** Euphemism (ID: `33268@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`)

## Daniel 11:35 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_e2mi)

כִּי

**"For"**

The word translated as **For** indicates that what follows is a reason for what came before. Use a connector in your language that makes it clear that what follows is a reason for what came before. Alternate translation: \[This is because]

See: Connect — Reason\-and\-Result Relationship

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:35
* **Associated Articles:** Connect — Reason-and-Result Relationship (ID: `33331@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`)

## Daniel 11:35 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_huft)

לִ⁠צְר֥וֹף בָּ⁠הֶ֛ם וּ⁠לְ⁠בָרֵ֥ר וְ⁠לַ⁠לְבֵּ֖ן

**"to refine and to purify and to whiten them"**

Here the angel is speaking as if these people were metal or cloth that someone would **refine** and **whiten**. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[so that they may become completely faithful and free from any fault]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 11:35
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`)

## Daniel 11:36 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_d3mx)

וְ⁠עָשָׂ֨ה כִ⁠רְצוֹנ֜⁠וֹ הַ⁠מֶּ֗לֶךְ

**"And the king will act according to his desire"**

The implication is that **the king** here is the king of the north, whom the angel has been describing. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[And the king of the north will act according to his desire]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 11:36 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_en7x)

וְ⁠יִתְרוֹמֵ֤ם וְ⁠יִתְגַּדֵּל֙

**"And he will exalt himself and magnify himself"**

The terms **exalt himself** and **magnify himself** mean similar things. The angel is using the two terms together for emphasis. If it would be clearer for your readers, you could express the emphasis with a single phrase. Alternate translation: \[And he will greatly glorify himself]

**"And to the gods of his fathers and to the desire of women, he will not attend"**

## Daniel 11:37 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_lg9e)

**"And to the gods of his fathers and to the desire of women, he will not attend"**

The angel is using **he will not attend** as a common expression of the culture to mean “he will show no regard.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. ## Daniel - Chapter 12 Introduction (id: en_tn_DAN_ashz)

Daniel 12 Introduction
======================

Structure and Formatting
------------------------

This chapter concludes the last section in the book, in which Daniel has a vision and man\-like figure appears to him and interprets it.

1. The man finishes his interpretation of Daniel’s vision (1–4\)
2. The man answers the question of when these things will happen (5–12\)

Translation issues in this chapter
----------------------------------

### How should translators represent the time periods in this chapter?

When two other figures ask the man who has been speaking with Daniel, “Until when will be the end of the wonders?” (12:6\), he replies that it will be “for an appointed time, appointed times, and a half” (12:7\). Some interpreters understand the term “time” to mean a “year” and the term “times” to mean “two times” (thus “two years”), and so some translations represent this expression as “three and a half years.” However, it is not certain that these are the meanings, and so we recommend that translators represent this expression in a way that allows for the range of possible interpretations. The UST models one way to do this. It translates this expression as “a time, times, and half a time.” We similarly recommend that translators represent the expressions “1,290 days” (12:11\) and “1,335 days” (12:12\) straightforwardly, without suggesting further interpretations of them.

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:0


## Daniel 12:1 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_ouy5)

וּ⁠בָ⁠עֵ֣ת הַ⁠הִיא֩

**"And at that time"**

The words that the ULT translates as **And at that time** indicate that the events of this verse happen at the same time as the events that the angel described in the previous chapter. If it would be helpful to your readers, you could describe the time relationship more explicitly. Alternate translation: \[And while those things are happening]

See: Connect — Simultaneous Time Relationship

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:1
* **Associated Articles:** Connect — Simultaneous Time Relationship (ID: `33334@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Michael (ID: `person:Michael.11`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`)

* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Michael (ID: `person:Michael.11`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`)

## Daniel 12:1 (#9) (id: en_tn_DAN_zt6e)

כָּל־הַ⁠נִּמְצָ֖א כָּת֥וּב בַּ⁠סֵּֽפֶר

**"every one found written in the book"**

The implication is that **the book** is a heavenly register in which God has recorded the people who belong to him. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[every one whose name God has written in his heavenly book]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:1
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Michael (ID: `person:Michael.11`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`)

## Daniel 12:2 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_wh7x)

מִ⁠יְּשֵׁנֵ֥י אַדְמַת־עָפָ֖ר יָקִ֑יצוּ

**"the sleepers of the ground of the dust will awaken"**

The angel is referring to dying and to coming back to life in a polite way by using the phrase **the sleepers of the ground of the dust will awaken**. Depending on what is helpful for your readers, you could use a polite way of referring to this in your language, or you could state this plainly. Alternate translation: \[those who are dead and buried in the ground will come back to life]

See: Euphemism

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:2
* **Associated Articles:** Euphemism (ID: `33268@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 12:2 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_vmqq)

לַ⁠חֲרָפ֖וֹת לְ⁠דִרְא֥וֹן עוֹלָֽם

**"to reproaches, to contempt of eternity"**

The terms **reproaches** and **contempt** mean similar things. The angel is using the two terms together for emphasis. If it would be clearer for your readers, you could express the emphasis with a single phrase. Alternate translation: \[to everlasting disgrace]

See: Doublet

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:2
* **Associated Articles:** Doublet (ID: `33266@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 12:2 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_qblt)

לְ⁠דִרְא֥וֹן עוֹלָֽם

**"to contempt of eternity"**

If your language does not use an abstract noun for the idea of **contempt**, you could express the same idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[so that people will despise them forever]

See: Abstract Nouns

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:2
* **Associated Articles:** Abstract Nouns (ID: `33257@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 12:3 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_yc7j)

וְ⁠הַ֨⁠מַּשְׂכִּלִ֔ים

**"And the ones making wise"**

The implication is that **the ones making wise** refers to either (1\) those who are wise themselves or (2\) those who make others wise. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[And those who are wise]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:3
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`)

## Daniel 12:3 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_cu8h)

יַזְהִ֖רוּ כְּ⁠זֹ֣הַר הָ⁠רָקִ֑יעַ

**"will shine like the brightness of the firmament"**

The point of this comparison is that just as **the brightness of the firmament** is radiant and glorious, so are **the ones making wise**. If it would be helpful in your language, you could make this point explicitly. Alternate translation: \[will be as radiant and glorious as the shining sky]

See: Simile

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:3
* **Associated Articles:** Simile (ID: `33314@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`)

## Daniel 12:3 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_gm1e)

כְּ⁠זֹ֣הַר הָ⁠רָקִ֑יעַ

**"like the brightness of the firmament"**

If your language does not use an abstract noun for the idea of **brightness**, you could express the same idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[like the bright sky above]

See: Abstract Nouns

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:3
* **Associated Articles:** Abstract Nouns (ID: `33257@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`)

## Daniel 12:3 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_se8d)

וּ⁠מַצְדִּיקֵי֙ הָֽ⁠רַבִּ֔ים כַּ⁠כּוֹכָבִ֖ים לְ⁠עוֹלָ֥ם וָ⁠עֶֽד

**"and the ones making many righteous, like stars forever and ever"**

The angel is leaving out some of the words that in many languages a sentence would need in order to be complete. You can supply these words from the context if that would be clearer in your language. Alternate translation: \[and the ones making many righteous will shine like stars forever and ever]

See: Ellipsis

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:3
* **Associated Articles:** Ellipsis (ID: `33267@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`)

## Daniel 12:3 (#5) (id: en_tn_DAN_p5ax)

וּ⁠מַצְדִּיקֵי֙ הָֽ⁠רַבִּ֔ים

**"and the ones making many righteous"**

The implication is that **the ones making many righteous** are people who lead many others to live rightly before God. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[and those who turn many people to righteousness]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:3
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`)

## Daniel 12:4 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_jkqg)

סְתֹ֧ם הַ⁠דְּבָרִ֛ים וַ⁠חֲתֹ֥ם הַ⁠סֵּ֖פֶר

**"close up the words, and seal the book"**

The implication is that to **close up the words, and seal the book** means to keep its contents secret and unaltered until the proper time. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[keep these words secret and preserve this scroll unchanged]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:4
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); Daniel (ID: `person:Daniel.3`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`)

## Daniel 12:4 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_dg4a)

יְשֹׁטְט֥וּ רַבִּ֖ים וְ⁠תִרְבֶּ֥ה הַ⁠דָּֽעַת

**"Many will go about, that knowledge may increase"**

Here, **that** marks **knowledge may increase** as the goal or purpose of **Many will go about**. Use a connector in your language that makes it clear that this is the purpose. Alternate translation: \[Many will travel about while trying to increase knowledge]

See: Connect — Goal (Purpose) Relationship

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:4
* **Associated Articles:** Connect — Goal (Purpose) Relationship (ID: `33330@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); Daniel (ID: `person:Daniel.3`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`)

## Daniel 12:4 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_ku71)

יְשֹׁטְט֥וּ רַבִּ֖ים

**"Many will go about"**

The angel is using **Many will go about** as a common expression of the culture to mean either (1\) “many people will travel here and there” or (2\) “many people will search through the book.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[Many will travel here and there]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:4
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); Daniel (ID: `person:Daniel.3`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`)

## Daniel 12:4 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_p4wh)

וְ⁠תִרְבֶּ֥ה הַ⁠דָּֽעַת

**"that knowledge may increase"**

If your language does not use an abstract noun for the idea of **knowledge**, you could express the same idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[that people may know more and more]

See: Abstract Nouns

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:4
* **Associated Articles:** Abstract Nouns (ID: `33257@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); Daniel (ID: `person:Daniel.3`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`)

## Daniel 12:5 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_sp4r)

שְׁנַ֥יִם אֲחֵרִ֖ים עֹמְדִ֑ים

**"two others were standing"**

Daniel is introducing **two others** as new participants in the story. Your language may have its own way of introducing new participants that you can use in your translation. Alternate translation: \[there were two other beings standing]

See: Introduction of New and Old Participants

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:5
* **Associated Articles:** Introduction of New and Old Participants (ID: `33421@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Daniel (ID: `person:Daniel.3`)

## Daniel 12:5 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_y4tu)

שְׁנַ֥יִם אֲחֵרִ֖ים

**"two others"**

The implication is that **two others** are two more angelic beings, in addition to the man clothed in linens whom Daniel had already seen. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[two other angelic beings]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:5
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Daniel (ID: `person:Daniel.3`)

## Daniel 12:5 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_f4kn)

אֶחָ֥ד הֵ֨נָּה֙ לִ⁠שְׂפַ֣ת הַ⁠יְאֹ֔ר

**"one here on the lip of the river"**

Daniel is speaking as if the edge of the river were a **lip**. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[one here on the bank of the river]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:5
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Daniel (ID: `person:Daniel.3`)

## Daniel 12:6 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_f1hb)

לָ⁠אִישׁ֙ לְב֣וּשׁ הַ⁠בַּדִּ֔ים

**"to the man clothed {in} linens"**

The implication is that **the man clothed {in} linens** is the angelic being who had been speaking to Daniel, identified here by his garment. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[to the angel who had been speaking to me, the man clothed in linens]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:6
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Daniel 12:6 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_cgt6)

אֲשֶׁ֥ר מִ⁠מַּ֖עַל לְ⁠מֵימֵ֣י הַ⁠יְאֹ֑ר

**"who {was} from above to the waters of the river"**

The implication is that the phrase **from above to the waters of the river** means that the man was positioned in the air over the river. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[who was hovering in the air over the waters of the river]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:6
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Daniel 12:6 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_xjev)

קֵ֥ץ הַ⁠פְּלָאֽוֹת

**"will be} the end of the wonders"**

The implication is that **the wonders** are the astonishing events that the angel described in this vision. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[will be the end of the astonishing events described in this vision]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:6
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Daniel 12:7 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_jjd0)

וַ⁠יָּ֨רֶם יְמִינ֤⁠וֹ וּ⁠שְׂמֹאל⁠וֹ֙ אֶל־הַ⁠שָּׁמַ֔יִם

**"And he raised his right hand and his left hand toward the heavens"**

Raising both hands toward heaven was a symbolic action by which the man clothed in linens showed that he was calling on God as the witness to the oath he was about to swear. If it would be helpful to your readers, you could explain the significance of this action in the text of your translation or in a footnote. Alternate translation: \[And he raised his right hand and his left hand toward the heavens to call on God as his witness]

See: Symbolic Action

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:7
* **Associated Articles:** Symbolic Action (ID: `33402@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Daniel 12:7 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_vdn5)

וַ⁠יִּשָּׁבַ֖ע בְּ⁠חֵ֣י הָ⁠עוֹלָ֑ם

**"and he swore by the one living {for} eternity"**

Following the custom of the culture, the man clothed in linens is swearing an oath and guaranteeing it by someone whom he holds sacred, **the one living {for} eternity**. Alternate translation: \[and he made a solemn promise, calling on the one who lives for eternity as his witness]

See: Oath Formulas

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Daniel 12:7 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_uypn)

בְּ⁠חֵ֣י הָ⁠עוֹלָ֑ם

**"by the one living {for} eternity"**

The implication is that **the one living {for} eternity** is a title for God. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[by God, the one who lives eternally]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:7
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Daniel 12:7 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_u4y5)

לְ⁠מוֹעֵ֨ד מֽוֹעֲדִ֜ים וָ⁠חֵ֗צִי

**"for an appointed time, appointed times, and a half"**

The man clothed in linens is using **an appointed time, appointed times, and a half** as a common expression of the culture to mean “a limited period whose length God has determined.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[for a limited period of time that God has determined]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:7
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Daniel 12:7 (#5) (id: en_tn_DAN_vy84)

וּ⁠כְ⁠כַלּ֛וֹת נַפֵּ֥ץ

**"And according to the finishing of the shattering of"**

If your language does not use abstract nouns for the ideas of **the finishing** and **the shattering**, you could express the same ideas in another way. Alternate translation: \[And when they finish shattering]

See: Abstract Nouns

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:7
* **Associated Articles:** Abstract Nouns (ID: `33257@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Daniel 12:7 (#6) (id: en_tn_DAN_kt60)

יַד־עַם־קֹ֖דֶשׁ

**"the hand of the people of holiness"**

Here, **the hand** represents “power.” If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression or plain language. Alternate translation: \[the power of the people of holiness]

See: Metonymy

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:7
* **Associated Articles:** Metonymy (ID: `33293@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Daniel 12:7 (#7) (id: en_tn_DAN_fb34)

עַם־קֹ֖דֶשׁ

**"the people of holiness"**

The man clothed in linens is using the possessive form to describe a **people** that is characterized by **holiness**. If a speaker of your language would not use the possessive form for this, you could express this idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[the holy people]

See: Possession

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:7
* **Associated Articles:** Possession (ID: `33303@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Daniel 12:7 (#8) (id: en_tn_DAN_h7w4)

תִּכְלֶ֥ינָה כָל־אֵֽלֶּה

**"all of these would finish"**

The pronoun **these** refers to the wonders that the other angel asked about in the previous verse. It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[all of these wonders would finish]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:7
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Daniel 12:8 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_kywb)

וַ⁠אֲנִ֥י שָׁמַ֖עְתִּי

**"And I, I heard"**

Daniel expresses the pronoun **I** twice here, where it would ordinarily be expressed only once. This suggests that he is emphasizing his own part in what happened. It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[And as for me, I myself heard]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:8
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Daniel 12:8 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_oz1b)

אֲדֹנִ֕⁠י

**"My lord"**

Daniel is referring to the man clothed in linens by a respectful title. Use a comparable title or expression for addressing someone respectfully in your language. Alternate translation: \[Honored sir]

See: Politeness

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:8
* **Associated Articles:** Politeness (ID: `33423@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Daniel 12:9 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_rfj8)

כִּֽי

**"for"**

The word translated as **for** indicates that what follows is a reason for what came before. Use a connector in your language that makes it clear that what follows is a reason for what came before. Alternate translation: \[because]

See: Connect — Reason\-and\-Result Relationship

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:9
* **Associated Articles:** Connect — Reason-and-Result Relationship (ID: `33331@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Daniel (ID: `person:Daniel.3`)

## Daniel 12:9 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_ksuv)

לֵ֣ךְ דָּנִיֵּ֑אל

**"Go, Daniel"**

The man clothed in linens is using **Go, Daniel** as a common expression of the culture to mean “go on your way and live out the rest of your life.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[Continue living your life, Daniel]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:9
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Daniel (ID: `person:Daniel.3`)

## Daniel 12:9 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_ur5e)

סְתֻמִ֧ים וַ⁠חֲתֻמִ֛ים הַ⁠דְּבָרִ֖ים

**"the words are closed up and sealed"**

The expressions **closed up** and **sealed** are passive verbal forms. If it would be unnatural to use those forms, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[God has closed up and sealed the words]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:9
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Daniel (ID: `person:Daniel.3`)

## Daniel 12:10 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_vg4j)

יִ֠תְבָּֽרֲרוּ וְ⁠יִֽתְלַבְּנ֤וּ וְ⁠יִצָּֽרְפוּ֙ רַבִּ֔ים

**"Many will be purified and whitened and refined"**

The expressions **purified**, **whitened**, and **refined** are passive verbal forms. If it would be unnatural to use those forms, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[God will purify and whiten and refine many]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:10
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); To Act Wickedly (ID: `keyterm:ActWickedly`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Daniel 12:10 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_x651)

יִ֠תְבָּֽרֲרוּ וְ⁠יִֽתְלַבְּנ֤וּ וְ⁠יִצָּֽרְפוּ֙ רַבִּ֔ים

**"Many will be purified and whitened and refined"**

The man clothed in linens is speaking as if these people were cloth that could be **purified and whitened** and metal that could be **refined**. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[Many will be made morally clean and pure and proven genuine]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:10
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); To Act Wickedly (ID: `keyterm:ActWickedly`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Daniel 12:10 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_sgwf)

וְ⁠הִרְשִׁ֣יעוּ רְשָׁעִ֔ים

**"but the wicked will act wickedly"**

The man clothed in linens is using the adjective **wicked** as a noun to mean a certain kind of person. Your language may use adjectives in the same way. If not, you can translate this adjective with an equivalent phrase. Alternate translation: \[but people who are wicked will act wickedly]

See: Nominal Adjectives

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:10
* **Associated Articles:** Nominal Adjectives (ID: `33294@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); To Act Wickedly (ID: `keyterm:ActWickedly`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Daniel 12:10 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_wc35)

וְ⁠לֹ֥א יָבִ֖ינוּ כָּל־רְשָׁעִ֑ים וְ⁠הַ⁠מַּשְׂכִּלִ֖ים יָבִֽינוּ

**"And not any of the wicked will understand, but the ones making wise will understand"**

The implication is that what the wicked will not **understand**, and what **the ones making wise will understand**, is the meaning of this message about the last days. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[And none of the wicked will understand the meaning of this message about the last days, but the people who are wise will understand it]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:10
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Daniel 12:11 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_n1kv)

וּ⁠מֵ⁠עֵת֙ הוּסַ֣ר הַ⁠תָּמִ֔יד וְ⁠לָ⁠תֵ֖ת שִׁקּ֣וּץ שֹׁמֵ֑ם יָמִ֕ים אֶ֖לֶף מָאתַ֥יִם וְ⁠תִשְׁעִֽים

**"And from the time of {when} the perpetual sacrifice is removed and the setting up of the abomination making desolate {will be} 1,290 days"**

If it would be more natural in your language, you may want to put the information about the **1,290 days** before the information about the time **when the perpetual sacrifice is removed and the setting up of the abomination making desolate**. Alternate translation: \[And there will be 1,290 days from the time of when the perpetual sacrifice is removed and the setting up of the abomination making desolate]

See: Information Structure

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:11
* **Associated Articles:** Information Structure (ID: `33286@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Detested Thing (ID: `keyterm:DetestedThing`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Daniel 12:11 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_w37a)

הוּסַ֣ר הַ⁠תָּמִ֔יד

**"when} the perpetual sacrifice is removed"**

The expression **is removed** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[when they remove the perpetual sacrifice]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:11
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Detested Thing (ID: `keyterm:DetestedThing`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Daniel 12:11 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_tvwn)

הַ⁠תָּמִ֔יד

**"the perpetual sacrifice"**

The implication is that **the perpetual sacrifice** is the offering that the priests presented in the temple every morning and every evening. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[the daily temple sacrifice]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:11
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Detested Thing (ID: `keyterm:DetestedThing`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Daniel 12:11 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_xzdp)

שִׁקּ֣וּץ שֹׁמֵ֑ם

**"the abomination making desolate"**

The implication is that **the abomination making desolate** is an idolatrous object that would defile the temple and cause people to abandon it. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[the detestable idol that defiles the temple and causes it to be abandoned]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:11
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Detested Thing (ID: `keyterm:DetestedThing`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Daniel 12:12 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_no4q)

אַשְׁרֵ֥י הַֽ⁠מְחַכֶּ֖ה

**"The happinesses of the one waiting"**

If your language does not use an abstract noun for the idea of **happinesses**, you could express the same idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[How happy is the one waiting]

See: Abstract Nouns

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:12
* **Associated Articles:** Abstract Nouns (ID: `33257@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 12:12 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_mrcx)

וְ⁠יַגִּ֑יעַ לְ⁠יָמִ֕ים אֶ֕לֶף שְׁלֹ֥שׁ מֵא֖וֹת שְׁלֹשִׁ֥ים וַ⁠חֲמִשָּֽׁה

**"and he reaches to the 1,335 days"**

The man clothed in linens is using **reaches to** as a common expression of the culture to mean “survives until.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[and he survives until the 1,335 days]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:12
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Daniel 12:13 (#1) (id: en_tn_DAN_oq5q)

לֵ֣ךְ לַ⁠קֵּ֑ץ

**"go until the end"**

The implication is that **the end** refers to either (1\) the end of Daniel’s own life or (2\) the end of the age. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[go until the end of your life]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:13
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `keyterm:Lot`)

## Daniel 12:13 (#2) (id: en_tn_DAN_xvhy)

וְ⁠תָנ֛וּחַ

**"for you will rest"**

The man clothed in linens is referring to Daniel’s death in a polite way by using the phrase **you will rest**. Depending on what is helpful for your readers, you could use a polite way of referring to this in your language, or you could state this plainly. Alternate translation: \[for you will pass away]

See: Euphemism

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:13
* **Associated Articles:** Euphemism (ID: `33268@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `keyterm:Lot`)

## Daniel 12:13 (#3) (id: en_tn_DAN_q3as)

וְ⁠תַעֲמֹ֥ד

**"and you will stand"**

The man clothed in linens is speaking as if Daniel’s return to life were his getting up to **stand**. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[and you will rise to life again]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:13
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `keyterm:Lot`)

## Daniel 12:13 (#4) (id: en_tn_DAN_zb16)

לְ⁠גֹרָלְ⁠ךָ֖

**"for your lot"**

Here, **your lot** represents “the portion that God has assigned to you.” If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression or plain language. Alternate translation: \[for the portion God has assigned to you]

See: Metonymy

* **Associated Passages:** Daniel 12:13
* **Associated Articles:** Metonymy (ID: `33293@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `keyterm:Lot`)
